The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *provision* is thus: the *provision* is the *giving*, the *count*, and the *diagnosis* together, and the *three* are the *condition* of the *gratitude*.

**The Modern Application: The Psychology of Habituation**

The *verse's* teaching on the *impossibility* of the *count* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *cannot count* the *blessings* is the *person* who *is habituated* to the *blessings*. The *one* who *takes* the *blessings* for *granted* is the *one* who *cannot count* them, and the *one* who *cannot count* them is the *one* who *cannot thank* them. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *habituation* is thus: the *habituation* is the *condition* of the *ingratitude*, and the *ingratitude* is the *condition* of the *diagnosis*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:35

> **And [remember] when Abraham said, "My Lord! Make this land secure, and keep me and my children from worshipping idols.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And [Remember] When Abraham Said": The Shift to the Father of the MonotTheists**

The verse shifts the scene from the *description* of the *disbelievers* (vv. 28–30) and the *command* to the *believers* (v. 31) to the *supplication* of *Abraham* (Ibrāhīm), the *father* of the *monotTheists*. The *word* *idhā* — "[remember] when" — is the *word* of the *recalled scene*, the *word* of the *one* who *calls to mind* the *scene* that has *already* occurred. The *sūrah* is *inviting* the *hearer* to *recall* the *supplication* of *Abraham*, and the *recall* is the *condition* of the *recognition*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *shift* is thus: the *shift* is the *condition* of the *recognition*, and the *recognition* is the *purpose* of the *supplication*.

**The Occasion of the Supplication: Hagar and Ishmael in Makkah**

The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) note that *when Hagar gave birth to Ishmael, Sarah became jealous of her; so Abraham took Hagar and Ishmael from Syria to Makkah*. The *word* *this land* (*hādhihi l-balad*) therefore refers to *Makkah*. The *occasion* of the *supplication* is the *arrival* of *Hagar* and *Ishmael* in *Makkah*, and the *arrival* is the *condition* of the *supplication*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *occasion* is thus: the *occasion* is the *arrival* of *Hagar* and *Ishmael* in *Makkah*, and the *arrival* is the *condition* of the *supplication*.

**"Make This Land Secure": The Prayer for the External Security**

The *first* *request* of the *supplication* — *ijʿal hādhihi l-balada āmanā* — "Make this land secure" — is the *prayer* for the *external* security. The *word* *āmanā* — "secure" — is the *word* of the *safety*, the *word* of the *state* in which there is *no fear*, *no harm*, *no danger*. The *prayer* is for the *safety* of the *land*, and the *safety* of the *land* is the *condition* of the *safety* of the *people*. The *commentators* (Ibn Kathīr) see the *answer* to *Abraham's* prayer that *Makkah* be *made secure* in the *wording* of 29:67:

> "Or have they not considered that We have made a secure sanctuary while people are snatched away all around it?" (29:67)

The *logic* of the *request* is the *logic* of the *external security*: the *safety* of the *land* is the *condition* of the *safety* of the *people*, and the *safety* of the *people* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *external security* is thus: the *external security* is the *condition* of the *safety* of the *people*, and the *safety* of the *people* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**"Keep Me and My Children from Worshipping Idols": The Prayer for the Internal Security**

The *second* *request* of the *supplication* — *wa-ʿizzirnī wa-zūrīya ʿan ʿibādati l-awṭās* — "and keep me and my children from worshipping idols" — is the *prayer* for the *internal* security. The *word* *ʿan ʿibādati l-awṭās* — "from worshipping idols" — is the *word* of the *shirk*, the *word* of the *one* who *worships* other than *God*. The *commentators* (al-Ṭūsī) explain that *Abraham is saying*, *"Make me and my children totally distant from idol worship."* The *prayer* is for the *distance* from *idol worship*, and the *distance* is the *condition* of the *purity* of the *faith*. The *logic* of the *request* is the *logic* of the *internal security*: the *distance* from *idol worship* is the *condition* of the *purity* of the *faith*, and the *purity* of the *faith* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *internal security* is thus: the *internal security* is the *condition* of the *purity* of the *faith*, and the *purity* of the *faith* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**The Two Dimensions of the Security: External and Internal**

The *two* *requests* together state the *full scope of the security*. The *first request* is the *external* security (the *safety* of the *land*), and the *second request* is the *internal* security (the *distance* from *idol worship*). The *two* dimensions together state the *full scope of the security*: the *security* is *both* *external* (the *safety* of the *land*) and *internal* (the *purity* of the *faith*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *security* is thus: the *security* is the *combination* of the *external* and the *internal*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**The Logic: The Land and the Family**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *security*. The *first aspect* is the *land*: the *safety* of the *land* is the *condition* of the *safety* of the *people*. The *second aspect* is the *family*: the *distance* from *idol worship* is the *condition* of the *purity* of the *faith*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the security*: the *security* is the *safety* of the *land* (the *first*) and the *purity* of the *faith* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *security* is thus: the *security* is the *combination* of the *land* and the *family*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**The Modern Application: The Two Dimensions of the Security**

The *verse's* teaching on the *two dimensions* of the *security* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *prays* for the *security* of the *land* is the *person* who *prays* for the *security* of the *community*, and the *person* who *prays* for the *security* of the *family* is the *person* who *prays* for the *security* of the *faith*. The *two* dimensions together state the *full scope of the security*: the *security* of the *community* and the *security* of the *faith*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *security* is thus: the *security* is the *combination* of the *community* and the *faith*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:36

> **My Lord! Surely they have led astray many among mankind. So whosoever follows me, he is of me. And whosoever disobeys me, surely Thou art Forgiving, Merciful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Surely They Have Led Astray Many Among Mankind": The Cause of the Misguidance**

The verse continues *Abraham's* supplication, and the continuation is the *statement* of the *cause* of the *misguidance*. The clause *qad ḍallat kathīran mina n-nās* — "Surely they have led astray many among mankind" — is the *word* of the *misguidance*. The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *phrase* is a *figurative expression* used to *indicate* the *manner* in which *people have been misguided in their service to lifeless idols*. The *misguidance* is *not* a *physical* *act*; the *misguidance* is the *service* to *lifeless idols*, and the *service* to *lifeless idols* is the *condition* of the *misguidance*. The *parallel* at 71:24 states the *same* *principle* in the *context* of *Noah*:

> "My Lord, do not let the people who have done wrong be a cause for my destruction." (71:24, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *cause*: the *misguidance* is the *condition* of the *service* to *lifeless idols*, and the *service* to *lifeless idols* is the *condition* of the *misguidance*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *misguidance* is thus: the *misguidance* is the *service* to *lifeless idols*, and the *service* to *lifeless idols* is the *condition* of the *misguidance*.

**"So Whosoever Follows Me, He Is of Me": The Inclusion**

The *first* *response* of *Abraham* — *fa-man tabiʿanī fa-hū minnī* — "So whosoever follows me, he is of me" — is the *statement* of the *inclusion*. The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *phrase* means that *whoever follows Abraham in believing in God's Oneness belongs to his monotheistic creed*. The *one* who *follows* *Abraham* is the *one* who *belongs* to *Abraham*, and the *belonging* is the *condition* of the *inclusion*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *inclusion*: the *one* who *follows* is the *one* who *belongs*, and the *belonging* is the *condition* of the *inclusion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *inclusion* is thus: the *inclusion* is the *belonging* to the *creed* of *Abraham*, and the *belonging* is the *condition* of the *inclusion*.

**"And Whosoever Disobeys Me, Surely Thou Art Forgiving, Merciful": The Referral**

The *second* *response* of *Abraham* — *wa-man ʿāṣanī fa-innaka ghaffār raḥīm* — "And whosoever disobeys me, surely Thou art Forgiving, Merciful" — is the *statement* of the *referral*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Ibrāhīmī, al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *phrase* refers to the *teaching* that *any sin will be forgiven by God except for the ascription of partners unto Him*, as in 4:48:

> "Truly God forgives not that any partner be ascribed unto Him, but He forgives what is less than that for whomsoever He will, for whosoever ascribes partners unto God has surely fabricated a tremendous sin." (4:48)

The *alternate* reading (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Ibrāhīmī, al-Qurṭubī) is that the *phrase* can also *include* the *sin* of *ascribing partners unto God*, which, *according to some commentators*, would then *pertain to the time before God informed Abraham that He does not forgive people for ascribing partners unto Him*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *referral*: *Abraham* *does not* *take over* the *role* of the *forgiver*; *Abraham* *refers* the *disobedient* one to *God's* *mercy*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *referral* is thus: the *referral* is the *condition* of the *mercy*, and the *mercy* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*.

**The Logic: The Inclusion and the Referral**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *responses* of *Abraham*. The *first response* is the *inclusion*: the *one* who *follows* *Abraham* is the *one* who *belongs* to *Abraham*. The *second response* is the *referral*: the *one* who *disobeys* *Abraham* is the *one* who is *referred* to *God's* *mercy*. The *two* responses together state the *full scope of the response*: the *response* is *both* *inclusion* (the *first*) and *referral* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *response* is thus: the *response* is the *combination* of the *inclusion* and the *referral*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *leadership*.

**The Psychology of Leadership: The Inclusion and the Referral**

The *verse's* teaching on the *psychology* of *leadership* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *leader* who *includes* the *follower* is the *leader* who *refers* the *disobedient* one to *God's* *mercy*. The *leader* who *takes over* the *role* of the *forgiver* is the *leader* who *breaks* the *referral*, and the *leader* who *breaks* the *referral* is the *leader* who *occupies* the *place* of *God*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *leadership* is thus: the *leadership* is the *combination* of the *inclusion* and the *referral*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *dignity* of the *leader*.

**The Frame: The Supplication and the Response**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *supplication* and the *response*. The *supplication* is the *request* of *Abraham* to *God*, and the *response* is the *statement* of the *inclusion* and the *referral*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the supplication*: the *supplication* is the *request* (the *first*), and the *response* is the *statement* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *supplication* is thus: the *supplication* is the *combination* of the *request* and the *response*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:37

> **Our Lord! Verily I have settled some of my progeny in a valley without cultivation by Thy Sacred House, our Lord, that they might perform the prayer. So cause the hearts of some men to incline toward them, and provide them with fruits, that haply they may give thanks.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"I Have Settled Some of My Progeny in a Valley Without Cultivation": The Setting of the Scene**

The verse continues *Abraham's* supplication, and the continuation is the *statement* of the *setting*. The clause *sakkanta min dhurriyatī bi-wādin ghayri ziraʿin ʿindā baytika l-muḥarram* — "Verily I have settled some of my progeny in a valley without cultivation by Thy Sacred House" — is the *word* of the *setting*. The *commentators* (al-Kalbī) explain that *some of my progeny* can be a *reference to Abraham's son Ishmael*, and the *alternate* reading (al-Qurṭubī) is that the *phrase* refers to *Ishmael and his mother, Hagar*. The *word* *wādin ghayri ziraʿin* — "a valley without cultivation" — is a *reference to Makkah*, and the *word* *baytika l-muḥarram* — "Thy Sacred House" — is the *Kaʿbah*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *setting*: the *progeny* is *settled* in a *valley* *without cultivation*, and the *setting* is the *condition* of the *supplication*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *setting* is thus: the *setting* is the *condition* of the *supplication*, and the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**"That They Might Perform the Prayer": The Purpose of the Settlement**

The *purpose* of the *settlement* is stated in the *clause* *li-yuqīmū l-ṣalāh* — "that they might perform the prayer." The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) note that *prayer* here is said to *denote* "religion" in its entirety, in light of prayer's superior rank in religion. The *purpose* of the *settlement* is therefore something like *"so that they may uphold religion"* (see also 42:13). The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *purpose*: the *settlement* is *for* the *purpose* of the *establishment* of the *religion*, and the *purpose* is the *condition* of the *settlement*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *purpose* is thus: the *purpose* is the *establishment* of the *religion*, and the *establishment* of the *religion* is the *condition* of the *settlement*.

**"Cause the Hearts of Some Men to Incline Toward Them": The First Request**

The *first* *request* of the *supplication* — *wa-jibib ilayhim qulūban min an-nās* — "So cause the hearts of some men to incline toward them" — is the *prayer* for the *attraction* of the *hearts*. The *word* *ajibib* — "incline, attract" — is the *word* of the *attraction*, the *word* of the *one* who *draws* the *hearts* *toward*. The *prayer* is for the *attraction* of the *hearts* of *some men* toward *the progeny*, and the *attraction* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *logic* of the *request* is the *logic* of the *attraction*: the *attraction* of the *hearts* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*, and the *establishment* of the *religion* is the *condition* of the *settlement*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *attraction* is thus: the *attraction* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*, and the *establishment* of the *religion* is the *condition* of the *settlement*.

**"And Provide Them with Fruits": The Second Request**

The *second* *request* of the *supplication* — *wa-ritzqhum min ath-thamarāt* — "and provide them with fruits" — is the *prayer* for the *sustenance*. The *commentators* (Ibn Kathīr) note that *although Makkah did not have date trees, Abraham's prayer to God to provide Hagar and Ishmael with fruits was answered, since fruits came to Makkah from many outlying regions*. The *alternate* reading (al-Jāmiʿ) is that *Abraham's prayer was answered by the bestowal of abundant date palms upon the town of Ṭāʾif*, a neighboring region that had a great deal of historical and commercial contact with Makkah. The *logic* of the *request* is the *logic* of the *sustenance*: the *sustenance* is the *condition* of the *settlement*, and the *settlement* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *sustenance* is thus: the *sustenance* is the *condition* of the *settlement*, and the *settlement* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**"That Haply They May Give Thanks": The Purpose of the Two Requests**

The *final* clause — *laʿallahum yashkurūn* — "that haply they may give thanks" — is the *statement* of the *purpose* of the *two requests*. The *purpose* of the *attraction* and the *sustenance* is the *gratitude*: the *one* who is *attracted* and *provided* is the *one* who *gives thanks*, and the *gratitude* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *purpose*: the *purpose* of the *two requests* is the *gratitude*, and the *gratitude* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *purpose* is thus: the *purpose* is the *gratitude*, and the *gratitude* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**The Logic: The Chain of the Supplication**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *chain* of the *supplication*. The *chain* is *complete*: the *settlement* (the *setting*), the *attraction* of the *hearts* (the *first request*), the *sustenance* (the *second request*), and the *gratitude* (the *purpose*). The *four* *links* together state the *full scope of the supplication*: the *settlement* is the *condition* of the *attraction*, the *attraction* is the *condition* of the *sustenance*, the *sustenance* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*, and the *gratitude* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *chain* is thus: the *chain* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*, and the *establishment* of the *religion* is the *condition* of the *settlement*.

**The Modern Application: The Prayer for the Community**

The *verse's* teaching on the *chain* of the *supplication* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *prays* for the *community* is the *person* who *prays* for the *attraction* of the *hearts* and the *sustenance* of the *community*. The *one* who *prays* for the *attraction* of the *hearts* is the *one* who *prays* for the *success* of the *community*, and the *one* who *prays* for the *sustenance* is the *one* who *prays* for the *prosperity* of the *community*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *prayer* is thus: the *prayer* is the *condition* of the *success* of the *community*, and the *success* of the *community* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:38

> **Our Lord! Surely Thou knowest what we hide and what we disclose. And naught is hidden from God, on earth or in Heaven.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Surely Thou Knowest What We Hide and What We Disclose": The Declaration of the Knowledge**

The verse is *Abraham's* declaration of *God's* knowledge. The clause *inna ka bī-mā nukhfi wa-nubdī* — "Surely Thou knowest what we hide and what we disclose" — is the *word* of the *knowledge*. The *word* *nukhfi* — "we hide" — is the *word* of the *secret*, the *word* of the *one* who *conceals*. The *word* *nubdī* — "we disclose" — is the *word* of the *open*, the *word* of the *one* who *reveals*. The *knowledge* of *God* is *total*: the *one* who *hides* is *known*, and the *one* who *discloses* is *known*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *totality*: the *knowledge* of *God* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *supplication*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *knowledge* is thus: the *knowledge* of *God* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *supplication*.

**"And Naught Is Hidden from God, on Earth or in Heaven": The Scope of the Knowledge**

The *next* clause — "And naught is hidden from God, on earth or in Heaven" — is the *statement* of the *scope* of the *knowledge*. The *scope* of the *knowledge* is *total*: the *one* who *is on earth* is *known*, and the *one* who *is in heaven* is *known*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *scope*: the *scope* of the *knowledge* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *declaration*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *scope* is thus: the *scope* of the *knowledge* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *declaration*.

**The Logic: The Knowledge and the Supplication**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *knowledge*. The *first aspect* is the *totality*: the *knowledge* of *God* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *supplication*. The *second aspect* is the *scope*: the *scope* of the *knowledge* is *total*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *declaration*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the knowledge*: the *knowledge* is *total* in its *totality* (the *first*), and the *knowledge* is *total* in its *scope* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *knowledge* is thus: the *knowledge* is *total* in its *totality* and *scope*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *supplication*.

**The Tension: The Knowledge and the Necessity of the Supplication**

The *verse* states a *tension* that the *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī) handle with care. The *tension* is this: if *God knows* what the *one* *hides* and *discloses*, *on earth* and *in heaven*, *why* must the *one* *supplicate*? The *commentators* explain that *God knows more about what will benefit human beings than they do themselves, and He is more merciful to them than they are to themselves*. Despite this fact, *human beings must still beseech God, so that they may manifest their state of servanthood to Him and their need for His Mercy, and because it hastens the Divine Response to their needs*. The *logic* of the *explanation* is the *logic* of the *manifestation*: the *supplication* is *not* *information* for *God*; the *supplication* is the *manifestation* of the *state* of the *servant*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *supplication* is thus: the *supplication* is the *manifestation* of the *state* of the *servant*, and the *manifestation* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Modern Application: The Supplication as the Expression of the State**

The *verse's* teaching on the *tension* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *supplicates* is *not* the *person* who *informs* *God*; the *person* who *supplicates* is the *person* who *manifests* his *state* of *servanthood*. The *one* who *supplicates* is the *one* who *recognizes* his *need*, and the *one* who *recognizes* his *need* is the *one* who *manifests* his *state* of *servanthood*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *supplication* is thus: the *supplication* is the *expression* of the *state*, and the *expression* of the *state* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Declaration and the Supplication**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *declaration* and the *supplication*. The *declaration* is the *statement* of the *knowledge* of *God*, and the *supplication* is the *request* of the *servant*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *knowledge* of *God* is *total* (the *declaration*), and the *supplication* of the *servant* is *necessary* (the *supplication*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *declaration* and the *supplication*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:39

> **Praise be to God, Who bestowed upon me Ishmael and Isaac, in my old age. Truly my Lord is the Hearer of supplications.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Praise Be to God": The Expression of the Gratitude**

The verse is *Abraham's* expression of *gratitude*. The clause *ḥamdun li-llāhi* — "Praise be to God" — is the *word* of the *praise*, the *word* of the *one* who *praises*. The *praise* is the *response* to the *blessing*, and the *blessing* is the *condition* of the *praise*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *response*: the *praise* is the *response* to the *blessing*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *praise* is thus: the *praise* is the *response* to the *blessing*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*.

**"Who Bestowed Upon Me Ishmael and Isaac, in My Old Age": The Blessing**

The *blessing* is stated in the *clause* *alladhī wahaaba lī Ismāʿīla wa-Isḥāqa fī l-kibar* — "Who bestowed upon me Ishmael and Isaac, in my old age." The *word* *wahaaba* — "bestowed, gave as a gift" — is the *word* of the *gift*, the *word* of the *one* who *gives* without *expectation*. The *blessing* is the *gift* of *Ishmael* and *Isaac*, and the *gift* is the *condition* of the *praise*. The *commentators* note that *here Abraham thanks God for giving him Ishmael through Hagar and Isaac through Sarah, despite the fact that he was advanced in years when he had both children*. The *parallel* at 11:71–74 and 51:24–28 states the *glad tidings* of the *birth* of *Isaac* *given to Abraham and Sarah, who was also in her old age*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *gift*: the *gift* is the *condition* of the *praise*, and the *praise* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*. The *sūrah's teaching on the *nature* of the *gift* is thus: the *gift* is the *condition* of the *praise*, and the *praise* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*.

**"Truly My Lord Is the Hearer of Supplications": The Confirmation**

The *final* clause — *inna rabbī lawaʾ ad-duʿāʾ* — "Truly my Lord is the Hearer of supplications" — is the *statement* of the *confirmation*. The *word* *lawāʾ ad-duʿāʾ* — "the Hearer of supplications" — is the *word* of the *hearing*, the *word* of the *one* who *hears* the *supplication*. The *confirmation* is the *statement* that *God hears* the *supplication*, and the *hearing* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *confirmation*: the *hearing* of the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *response*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *blessing*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *confirmation* is thus: the *confirmation* is the *condition* of the *response*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *blessing*.

**The Logic: The Praise and the Confirmation**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *gratitude*. The *first aspect* is the *praise*: the *praise* is the *response* to the *blessing*. The *second aspect* is the *confirmation*: the *hearing* of the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the gratitude*: the *gratitude* is the *praise* (the *first*) and the *confirmation* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *gratitude* is thus: the *gratitude* is the *combination* of the *praise* and the *confirmation*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *blessing*.

**The Modern Application: The Praise as the Recognition of the Answer**

The *verse's* teaching on the *praise* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *praises* *God* is the *person* who *recognizes* the *answer* to the *supplication*. The *one* who *praises* is the *one* who *recognizes* that the *supplication* has *been answered*, and the *one* who *recognizes* the *answer* is the *one* who *praises*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *praise* is thus: the *praise* is the *recognition* of the *answer*, and the *recognition* of the *answer* is the *condition* of the *gratitude*.

**The Frame: The Supplication and the Praise**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *supplication* and the *praise*. The *supplication* (v. 37) is the *request* of *Abraham* to *God*, and the *praise* (v. 39) is the *response* of *Abraham* to *God*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *supplication* is the *request* (the *first*), and the *praise* is the *response* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *supplication* and the *praise*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *blessing*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:40

> **My Lord! Make me a performer of prayer, and my progeny. Our Lord! Accept my supplication!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Make Me a Performer of Prayer": The Request for the Firmness in the Religion**

The verse is *Abraham's* request for the *firmness* in the *religion*. The clause *ijʿalnī muqīm aṣ-alāh* — "Make me a performer of prayer" — is the *word* of the *request*. The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) explain that *a performer of prayer* can refer to *one who is firm in religion*, and the *alternate* reading (al-Bayḵāwī) is that the *phrase* refers to *one who is persistent in the actual act of prayer*. The *request* is for the *firmness* in the *religion*, and the *firmness* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *request*: the *request* is for the *firmness*, and the *firmness* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is for the *firmness* in the *religion*, and the *firmness* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion*.

**"And My Progeny": The Extension of the Request**

The *extension* of the *request* is stated in the *clause* *wa-min dhurriyatī* — "and my progeny." The *request* is *extended* to *Abraham's progeny*, and the *extension* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion* in the *progeny*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *extension*: the *request* is *extended* to the *progeny*, and the *extension* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion* in the *progeny*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *extension* is thus: the *extension* is the *condition* of the *establishment* of the *religion* in the *progeny*, and the *establishment* of the *religion* in the *progeny* is the *condition* of the *request*.

**"Accept My Supplication": The Request for the Acceptance**

The *final* clause — *wa-jaʿib duʿāʾī* — "Accept my supplication!" — is the *request* for the *acceptance*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī) explain that the *phrase* may be a *request* on the *part of Abraham* that *God accept his worship in general or his supplication as mentioned in this verse in particular*. The *request* for the *acceptance* is the *request* that *God accept* the *supplication*, and the *acceptance* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *request*: the *request* is for the *acceptance*, and the *acceptance* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is for the *acceptance*, and the *acceptance* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Prophetic Confirmation: The Supplication and the Haste**

The *Prophet* ﷺ is *reported* to have said of the *supplication*:

> "The supplication of one of you is not rejected until he is in haste, and says, 'I asked, and I asked, and I see that I have not been answered,' and then he gives up and abandons it." (Muslim 2755, from Ḥaḍḍāb ibn al-Mundsir)

The *report* *confirms* the *principle*: the *supplication* is *not rejected* until the *one* is *in haste*, and the *haste* is the *condition* of the *abandonment*. The *logic* of the *report* is the *logic* of the *patience*: the *patience* in the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*, and the *haste* is the *condition* of the *abandonment*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *supplication* is thus: the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*, and the *acceptance* is the *condition* of the *patience*.

**The Logic: The Request and the Acceptance**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *request*. The *first aspect* is the *firmness*: the *request* is for the *firmness* in the *religion*. The *second aspect* is the *acceptance*: the *request* is for the *acceptance* of the *supplication*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the request*: the *request* is for the *firmness* (the *first*) and the *acceptance* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is the *combination* of the *firmness* and the *acceptance*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Modern Application: The Supplication and the Patience**

The *verse's* teaching on the *supplication* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *supplicates* is the *person* who *is patient* in the *supplication*. The *one* who *is in haste* in the *supplication* is the *one* who *abandons* the *supplication*, and the *one* who *abandons* the *supplication* is the *one* who *misses* the *acceptance*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *supplication* is thus: the *patience* in the *supplication* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*, and the *acceptance* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Request and the Response**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *request* and the *response*. The *request* is the *supplication* of *Abraham* to *God*, and the *response* is the *acceptance* of *God*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *request* is the *condition* of the *response*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *request* and the *response*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:41

> **Our Lord! Forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day when the Reckoning is come."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Forgive Me and My Parents": The Request for the Forgiveness**

The verse is *Abraham's* request for the *forgiveness*. The clause *irḥamnī wa-wālidayya wa-ll-muʾminīna yawma yaqūmu l-ḥisāb* — "Forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day when the Reckoning is come" — is the *word* of the *request*. The *request* is for the *forgiveness* of *Abraham*, *his parents*, and *the believers*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *request*: the *request* is for the *forgiveness*, and the *forgiveness* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is for the *forgiveness*, and the *forgiveness* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*.

**"On the Day When the Reckoning Is Come": The Setting of the Request**

The *setting* of the *request* is the *Day of Judgment*: *yawma yaqūmu l-ḥisāb* — "on the Day when the Reckoning is come." The *request* is for the *forgiveness* on the *Day of Judgment*, and the *Day of Judgment* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *setting*: the *setting* is the *Day of Judgment*, and the *Day of Judgment* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *setting* is thus: the *setting* is the *Day of Judgment*, and the *Day of Judgment* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*.

**The Condition of the Forgiveness: The Submission of the Parents**

The *commentators* (al-Kalbī) note that *Abraham's supplication for the forgiveness of his parents is seen as a request to God that He forgive them on the condition that they submit to Him and believe in Him*. The *parallel* at 19:47 and 9:113 states the *principle* explicitly:

> "Abraham's plea for forgiveness of his father was only due to a promise he had made to him. But when it became clear to him that he was an enemy of God, he repudiated him." (9:114, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *condition* is the *logic* of the *submission*: the *forgiveness* of the *parents* is *conditional* upon the *submission* of the *parents*, and the *submission* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *condition* is thus: the *condition* is the *submission* of the *parents*, and the *submission* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*.

**The Alternate Readings: The Timing of the Supplication**

The *alternate* reading (al-Kalbī) is that *Abraham made the supplication for his parents before coming to know that his father was an enemy of God*. The *alternate* reading (al-Jāmiʿ) is that *both his parents were enemies of God*. The *alternate* reading (al-Jāmiʿ) is that an *alternate reading of this verse has* "my father" *instead of* "my parents." The *logic* of the *alternate readings* is the *logic* of the *timing*: the *supplication* is *made* *before* the *knowledge* of the *enemy* of *God*, and the *knowledge* of the *enemy* of *God* is the *condition* of the *repudiation*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *timing* is thus: the *timing* is the *condition* of the *repudiation*, and the *repudiation* is the *condition* of the *knowledge*.

**The Tension: The Father-Son Relationship and the Faith**

The *verse* states a *tension* that the *commentators* handle with care. The *tension* is this: *Abraham* *prays* for the *forgiveness* of his *parents*, but *Abraham* *repudiates* his *father* when it *becomes clear* that his *father* is an *enemy* of *God*. The *commentators* explain that the *supplication* is *conditional* upon the *submission* of the *parents*, and the *repudiation* is the *consequence* of the *knowledge* of the *enemy* of *God*. The *logic* of the *explanation* is the *logic* of the *condition*: the *supplication* is *conditional* upon the *submission*, and the *repudiation* is the *consequence* of the *knowledge*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *tension* is thus: the *tension* is resolved by the *condition*, and the *condition* is the *submission* of the *parents*.

**The Logic: The Request and the Condition**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *request*. The *first aspect* is the *request*: the *request* is for the *forgiveness* of *Abraham*, *his parents*, and *the believers*. The *second aspect* is the *condition*: the *forgiveness* of the *parents* is *conditional* upon the *submission* of the *parents*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the request*: the *request* is for the *forgiveness* (the *first*), and the *condition* is the *submission* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is the *combination* of the *forgiveness* and the *condition*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *acceptance*.

**The Modern Application: The Limits of Intercession**

The *verse's* teaching on the *limits* of *intercession* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *prays* for the *forgiveness* of his *parents* is the *person* who *prays* *on the condition* that his *parents* *submit* to *God*. The *one* who *prays* *unconditionally* is the *one* who *breaks* the *condition*, and the *one* who *breaks* the *condition* is the *one* who *occupies* the *place* of *God*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *intercession* is thus: the *intercession* is *conditional* upon the *submission*, and the *submission* is the *condition* of the *forgiveness*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:42

> **Do not suppose that God is heedless of the deeds of the wrongdoers; He merely grants them reprieve till a day when eyes will stare, transfixed,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Do Not Suppose That God Is Heedless of the Deeds of the Wrongdoers": The Admonition**

The verse shifts the scene from the *supplication* of *Abraham* (vv. 35–41) to the *warning* of the *wrongdoers*. The clause *lā taḥsabanna llāha ghāfilan ʿammā yaʿmalu ẓ-ẓālimūn* — "Do not suppose that God is heedless of the deeds of the wrongdoers" — is the *word* of the *admonition*. The *commentators* (al-Rāzī) explain that the *phrase* is understood to be an *admonition* that *God will exact vengeance upon the wrongdoers for their unjust actions*. The *logic* of the *admonition* is the *logic* of the *vengeance*: the *vengeance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*, and the *reckoning* is the *condition* of the *justice*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *admonition* is thus: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *vengeance*, and the *vengeance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Logic of the Admonition: The Three Impossibilities**

The *commentators* (al-Rāzī) explain the *logic* of the *admonition* in terms of the *three impossibilities*. The *logic* is this: if *God were not to exact vengeance* upon the *wrongdoers*, it would mean that *He is ignorant of their injustices, or is incapable of exacting vengeance upon them, or is pleased by their injustices*. Since *ignorance, incapacity, or pleasure in seeing injustice do not apply to God*, it is considered *impossible* that *He not take the wrongdoers to task for their wrong actions*. The *three impossibilities* together state the *full scope of the admonition*: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *vengeance*, and the *vengeance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *admonition* is thus: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *vengeance*, and the *vengeance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**"He Merely Grants Them Reprieve": The Reprieve**

The *clause* *innamā yumhīluhum* — "He merely grants them reprieve" — is the *word* of the *reprieve*. The *commentators* (al-Nīsābūrī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *God's granting reprieve from punishment*. The *reprieve* is *not* the *absence* of the *reckoning*; the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *delay*: the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*, and the *delay* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *reprieve* is thus: the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*, and the *delay* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**"Till a Day When Eyes Will Stare, Transfixed": The Day of the Reckoning**

The *final* clause — *li-yawmin tamtaʿriq fīhi l-abṣār* — "till a day when eyes will stare, transfixed" — is the *word* of the *Day*. The *commentators* (al-Nīsābūrī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *the Day of Judgment, when eyeballs come out of their sockets on account of the terrors that will be seen on the Last Day*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *terror*: the *terror* is the *condition* of the *staring*, and the *staring* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *Day* is thus: the *Day* is the *condition* of the *terror*, and the *terror* is the *condition* of the *staring*.

**The Cross-References: The Reprieve and the Appointed Term**

The *parallel* at 16:61 and 35:45 states the *same* *principle* of the *reprieve*: *God grants reprieve until an appointed term*. The *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*, and the *appointed term* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *reprieve* is thus: the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*, and the *delay* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Logic: The Admonition, the Reprieve, and the Day**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *three* *aspects* of the *warning*. The *first aspect* is the *admonition*: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *vengeance*. The *second aspect* is the *reprieve*: the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*. The *third aspect* is the *Day*: the *Day* is the *condition* of the *terror*. The *three* aspects together state the *full scope of the warning*: the *warning* is the *admonition* (the *first*), the *reprieve* (the *second*), and the *Day* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *warning* is thus: the *warning* is the *combination* of the *admonition*, the *reprieve*, and the *Day*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Modern Application: The Reprieve and the Reckoning**

The *verse's* teaching on the *reprieve* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *is granted* a *reprieve* is the *person* who *is not* *reckoned*. The *one* who *is not* *reckoned* is the *one* who *is delayed*, and the *one* who *is delayed* is the *one* who *will be reckoned*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *reprieve* is thus: the *reprieve* is the *delay* of the *reckoning*, and the *delay* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:43

> **running with necks outstretched and heads upraised, their glance returning not to them, their hearts vacant.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Running with Necks Outstretched": The State of the Humiliation**

The verse continues the *description* of the *Day* begun in v. 42. The *clause* *mukhbitīn* — "running with necks outstretched" — is the *word* of the *state*. The *commentators* (al-Ajfārī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *a state of humiliation and defeat, like slaves*. The *one* who *is running* with *necks outstretched* is the *one* who *is in the state* of *humiliation* and *defeat*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *humiliation*: the *humiliation* is the *condition* of the *running*, and the *running* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *humiliation* is thus: the *humiliation* is the *condition* of the *running*, and the *running* is the *condition* of the *Day*.

**"And Heads Upraught": The State of the Fear**

The *next* clause — *yajmāʿūna ruʾūsa* — "and heads upraised" — is the *word* of the *state* of the *fear*. The *commentators* (al-Ajfārī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *a state of intense fear or because of the chains around their necks* (see also 36:8). The *one* who *has* *heads upraised* is the *one* who *is in the state* of *intense fear*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *fear*: the *fear* is the *condition* of the *uprighting* of the *heads*, and the *uprighting* of the *heads* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *fear* is thus: the *fear* is the *condition* of the *uprighting* of the *heads*, and the *uprighting* of the *heads* is the *condition* of the *Day*.

**"Their Glance Returning Not to Them": The State of the Terror**

The *next* clause — "their glance returning not to them" — is the *word* of the *state* of the *terror*. The *commentators* (al-Ajfārī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *the one who will not be able to look at himself because of his extreme terror* or *because the chains around his neck will prevent this*. The *one* whose *glance* *does not return* to *him* is the *one* who *is in the state* of *extreme terror*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *terror*: the *terror* is the *condition* of the *glance* that *does not return*, and the *glance* that *does not return* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *terror* is thus: the *terror* is the *condition* of the *glance* that *does not return*, and the *glance* that *does not return* is the *condition* of the *Day*.

**"Their Hearts Vacant": The State of the Stupor**

The *final* clause — *wa-qulūbuhum khāwiyāt* — "their hearts vacant" — is the *word* of the *state* of the *stupor*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī) explain that the *phrase* refers to *a state in which the hearts are bereft of any kind of understanding, so consumed by their stupor*. The *one* whose *heart* is *vacant* is the *one* who *is in the state* of *stupor*, and the *stupor* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *stupor*: the *stupor* is the *condition* of the *vacancy* of the *hearts*, and the *vacancy* of the *hearts* is the *condition* of the *Day*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *stupor* is thus: the *stupor* is the *condition* of the *vacancy* of the *hearts*, and the *vacancy* of the *hearts* is the *condition* of the *Day*.

**The Logic: The Four States of the Day**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *four* *states* of the *Day*. The *first state* is the *humiliation*: the *humiliation* is the *condition* of the *running*. The *second state* is the *fear*: the *fear* is the *condition* of the *uprighting* of the *heads*. The *third state* is the *terror*: the *terror* is the *condition* of the *glance* that *does not return*. The *fourth state* is the *stupor*: the *stupor* is the *condition* of the *vacancy* of the *hearts*. The *four* states together state the *full scope of the Day*: the *Day* is the *humiliation* (the *first*), the *fear* (the *second*), the *terror* (the *third*), and the *stupor* (the *fourth*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *Day* is thus: the *Day* is the *combination* of the *four states*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Modern Application: The States of the Day**

The *verse's* teaching on the *four states* of the *Day* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *is in the state* of *humiliation* is the *person* who *is running* with *necks outstretched*. The *person* who *is in the state* of *fear* is the *person* who *has* *heads upraised*. The *person* who *is in the state* of *terror* is the *person* whose *glance* *does not return*. The *person* who *is in the state* of *stupor* is the *person* whose *heart* is *vacant*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *four states* is thus: the *four states* are the *conditions* of the *Day*, and the *Day* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Frame: The Description and the Reckoning**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *description* and the *reckoning*. The *description* is the *statement* of the *four states* of the *Day*, and the *reckoning* is the *consequence* of the *four states*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the Day*: the *Day* is the *description* (the *first*), and the *reckoning* is the *consequence* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *Day* is thus: the *Day* is the *combination* of the *description* and the *reckoning*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *justice*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:44

> **And warn mankind of the Day when the punishment shall come upon them, whereupon those who did wrong will say, "Our Lord! Grant us reprieve for a term nigh, that we might respond to Thy Call and follow the messengers." [It will be said unto them], "Did you not earlier swear that there would be no end for you?"**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And Warn Mankind of the Day When the Punishment Shall Come Upon Them": The Command to the Prophet**

The verse shifts the scene from the *description* of the *Day* (vv. 42–43) to the *command* to the *Prophet*. The clause *fa-udhar an-nāsa yawma yaʾtī l-ʿadhāb* — "And warn mankind of the Day when the punishment shall come upon them" — is the *word* of the *command*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Kalbī, al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *reference* here is to the *Day of Judgment*, and *by mankind is meant the Makkan idolaters specifically* (al-Qurṭubī). The *command* is to *warn* *mankind* of the *Day*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *command*: the *command* is to *warn*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *command* is thus: the *command* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**"Grant Us Reprieve for a Term Nigh": The Request of the Wrongdoers**

The *request* of the *wrongdoers* is stated in the *clause* *fa-akhkhirnā ilā qaribin* — "Grant us reprieve for a term nigh." The *request* is for a *short reprieve*, and the *reprieve* is the *condition* of the *response* to the *Call*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *request*: the *request* is for the *reprieve*, and the *reprieve* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *request* is thus: the *request* is the *condition* of the *reprieve*, and the *reprieve* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**"That We Might Respond to Thy Call and Follow the Messengers": The Purpose of the Request**

The *purpose* of the *request* is stated in the *clause* *najībūna li-daʿwatika wa-natabiʿa l-rusul* — "that we might respond to Thy Call and follow the messengers." The *purpose* of the *reprieve* is the *response* to the *Call* and the *following* of the *messengers*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *purpose*: the *purpose* of the *reprieve* is the *response* to the *Call*, and the *response* to the *Call* is the *condition* of the *following* of the *messengers*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *purpose* is thus: the *purpose* is the *response* to the *Call*, and the *response* to the *Call* is the *condition* of the *following* of the *messengers*.

**"Did You Not Earlier Swear That There Would Be No End for You?": The Answer**

The *answer* to the *request* is stated in the *clause* — "Did you not earlier swear that there would be no end for you?" The *answer* is the *statement* of the *oath* that the *wrongdoers* *swore* in this *world*: the *oath* that *there would be no end* for them. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *oath*: the *oath* is the *condition* of the *answer*, and the *answer* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *oath* is thus: the *oath* is the *condition* of the *answer*, and the *answer* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Cross-References: The Oath and the Resurrection**

The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, Ibn Kathīr, al-Qurṭubī, al-Zamakhsharī) connect the *last part* of this *verse* — *Did you not earlier swear that there would be no end for you?* — with the *Makkan idolaters'* statement mentioned at 16:38:

> "And they swear by God their most solemn oaths [that] God will not resurrect those who die." (16:38)

The *parallel* at 6:27–28 states the *same* *principle* of the *request*:

> "If thou couldst see when they are arraigned before the Fire, whereupon they will say, 'Would that we were sent back! Then we would not deny the signs of our Lord, but we would be among the believers!' Nay, but it has now become clear to them what they used to hide." (6:27–28, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *cross-references* is the *logic* of the *oath*: the *oath* is the *condition* of the *request*, and the *request* is the *condition* of the *answer*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *oath* is thus: the *oath* is the *condition* of the *request*, and the *request* is the *condition* of the *answer*.

**The Futility of the Request: The Logic of the Return**

The *request* is *futile* because the *one* who *is returned* *would return to the very thing he had been forbidden*. The *parallel* at 6:28 maintains that *even if they were returned to their life on earth to mend their ways, they would return to the very thing they had been forbidden. Truly they are liars*. The *logic* of the *futility* is the *logic* of the *liar*: the *one* who *swore* the *oath* is the *one* who *is a liar*, and the *one* who *is a liar* is the *one* who *would return* to the *very thing* he had *been forbidden*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *futility* is thus: the *futility* is the *condition* of the *liar*, and the *liar* is the *condition* of the *oath*.

**The Modern Application: The Psychology of the Last Chance**

The *verse's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *request* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *requests* a *last chance* is the *person* who *swore* the *oath* in this *world*. The *one* who *swore* the *oath* is the *one* who *is a liar*, and the *one* who *is a liar* is the *one* who *would return* to the *very thing* he had *been forbidden*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *last chance* is thus: the *last chance* is the *condition* of the *liar*, and the *liar* is the *condition* of the *oath*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:45

> **And you settled in the dwellings of those who had wronged themselves, though it was made clear to you how We dealt with them, and We set forth for you the parables.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"You Settled in the Dwellings of Those Who Had Wronged Themselves": The Inheritance of the Ruins**

The verse addresses the *Makkans* directly, and the address is the *statement* of the *fact*. The clause *wa-laqad wajadtum maʾsakan alladhīna ẓalamū anfusahum* — "And you settled in the dwellings of those who had wronged themselves" — is the *word* of the *fact*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *phrase* means that *the people of Makkah had settled in a land that had previously been inhabited by groups who rejected the prophets sent to them, such as the tribes of ʿĀd and Thamūd*. The *Makkans* *inherited* the *land* of the *destroyed peoples*, and the *inheritance* is the *condition* of the *lesson*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *inheritance*: the *inheritance* of the *land* is the *condition* of the *lesson*, and the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *inheritance* is thus: the *inheritance* is the *condition* of the *lesson*, and the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**"Though It Was Made Clear to You How We Dealt with Them": The Witness of the Ruins**

The *clause* — "though it was made clear to you how We dealt with them" — is the *statement* of the *witness*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī) explain that the *phrase* means that *the Makkans could still witness the vestiges of the destroyed peoples by looking at the ruins of their dwellings and hearing the stories about them that were passed down from generation to generation*. The *ruins* are the *witness* of the *dealing*, and the *witness* is the *condition* of the *lesson*. The *parallel* at 46:21–26 states the *same* *principle* of the *ruins*:

> "Are you more difficult to create, or the heaven above? He has reconstructed it and made it even. And He has spread out the earth and set therein firm mountains and rivers, and of every fruit two kinds. He covers the night with the day. Verily in that are signs for a people who reflect." (46:15–16, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *witness*: the *witness* of the *ruins* is the *condition* of the *lesson*, and the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *witness* is thus: the *witness* is the *condition* of the *lesson*, and the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**"And We Set Forth for You the Parables": The Parable in the Qur'an**

The *final* clause — *wa-qad sammaʿnā lakum al-amthāla* — "and We set forth for you the parables" — is the *statement* of the *parable*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Qurṭubī) explain that the *phrase* means that *in the Quran God shows that the Makkans were like these previous nations in their disbelief, and were likewise worthy of being punished*. The *parable* is the *statement* of the *similarity*, and the *similarity* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *parable*: the *parable* is the *condition* of the *similarity*, and the *similarity* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *parable* is thus: the *parable* is the *condition* of the *similarity*, and the *similarity* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**The Logic: The Inheritance, the Witness, and the Parable**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *three* *aspects* of the *warning*. The *first aspect* is the *inheritance*: the *inheritance* of the *land* is the *condition* of the *lesson*. The *second aspect* is the *witness*: the *witness* of the *ruins* is the *condition* of the *lesson*. The *third aspect* is the *parable*: the *parable* is the *condition* of the *similarity*. The *three* aspects together state the *full scope of the warning*: the *warning* is the *inheritance* (the *first*), the *witness* (the *second*), and the *parable* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *warning* is thus: the *warning* is the *combination* of the *inheritance*, the *witness*, and the *parable*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *lesson*.

**The Modern Application: The Lesson of History**

The *verse's* teaching on the *lesson* of *history* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *settles* in the *land* of the *destroyed peoples* is the *person* who *inherites* the *lesson*. The *one* who *witnesses* the *ruins* is the *one* who *learns* the *lesson*, and the *one* who *learns* the *lesson* is the *one* who *is warned*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *lesson* is thus: the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Ruins and the Warning**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *ruins* and the *warning*. The *ruins* are the *witness* of the *dealing*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *ruins* are the *condition* of the *lesson*, and the *lesson* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *ruins* and the *warning*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:46

> **And they devised their plot, but their plot lies with God, though their plotting be such as to move mountains.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And They Devised Their Plot": The Scheme of the Disbelievers**

The verse states the *scheme* of the *disbelievers*. The clause *wa-makarū makran* — "And they devised their plot" — is the *word* of the *scheme*. The *word* *makar* — "plot, scheme" — is the *word* of the *one* who *plots* against the *truth*, the *one* who *devises* a *scheme* to *buried* the *truth*. The *scheme* is the *condition* of the *response* of *God*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *scheme*: the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *response*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *dealing*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *scheme* is thus: the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *response*, and the *response* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**"But Their Plot Lies with God": The Knowledge and the Recompense**

The *clause* *wa-makruhū ʿindā llāh* — "but their plot lies with God" — is the *statement* of the *knowledge* and the *recompense*. The *commentators* (al-Rāzī) explain that the *phrase* means that *God knows either their plot* (al-Rāzī) *or the recompense for it* (al-Kalbī). The *plot* *lies with God* in the *sense* that *God knows* the *plot*, and the *plot* *lies with God* in the *sense* that *God will recompense* the *plot*. The *parallel* at 7:99, 10:21, 13:42, 27:50, and 86:15–16 states the *same* *principle*: the *plot* of the *disbelievers* is *known* to *God*, and the *plot* will be *recompensed* by *God*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *knowledge*: the *knowledge* of the *plot* is the *condition* of the *recompense*, and the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *dealing*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *knowledge* is thus: the *knowledge* is the *condition* of the *recompense*, and the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**"Though Their Plotting Be Such as to Move Mountains": The Impossibility of the Scheme**

The *final* clause — *law yakūnu makruhū yakhriju l-jibāl* — "though their plotting be such as to move mountains" — is the *statement* of the *impossibility*. The *commentators* (al-Zamakhsharī) note that the *phrase* implies that *no plot, no matter how great—even as great in scope as a mountain—can overcome the Will of God*. The *scheme* that *could move mountains* is *still* *insufficient* to *overcome* the *Will of God*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *impossibility*: the *scheme* that *could move mountains* is *insufficient*, and the *insufficiency* is the *condition* of the *impossibility*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *impossibility* is thus: the *impossibility* is the *condition* of the *insufficiency*, and the *insufficiency* is the *condition* of the *scheme*.

**The Mountains as the Signs of God: The Fixed and the Firmly Rooted**

The *commentators* (al-Kalbī, al-Zamakhsharī) note that *mountains are also understood as a reference to God's signs, the Sacred Law, and prophecy, since, like mountains, they are fixed and firmly rooted* (cf. vv. 24–25). The *mountains* are the *signs* of *God*, the *mountains* are the *sacred law*, the *mountains* are the *prophecy*. The *scheme* that *could move mountains* is the *scheme* that *could move the signs of God*, and the *impossibility* of the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *fixedness* of the *signs*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *fixedness*: the *fixedness* of the *signs* is the *condition* of the *impossibility*, and the *impossibility* is the *condition* of the *scheme*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *fixedness* is thus: the *fixedness* is the *condition* of the *impossibility*, and the *impossibility* is the *condition* of the *scheme*.

**The Logic: The Scheme, the Knowledge, and the Impossibility**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *three* *aspects* of the *scheme*. The *first aspect* is the *scheme*: the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *second aspect* is the *knowledge*: the *knowledge* of the *plot* is the *condition* of the *recompense*. The *third aspect* is the *impossibility*: the *impossibility* of the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *fixedness* of the *signs*. The *three* aspects together state the *full scope of the scheme*: the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *response* (the *first*), the *knowledge* is the *condition* of the *recompense* (the *second*), and the *impossibility* is the *condition* of the *fixedness* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *scheme* is thus: the *scheme* is the *combination* of the *three aspects*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**The Modern Application: The Psychology of the Scheme**

The *verse's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *scheme* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *devises* a *scheme* against the *truth* is the *person* who *plots* to *bury* the *truth*. The *one* who *plots* to *bury* the *truth* is the *one* whose *scheme* is *known* to *God*, and the *one* whose *scheme* is *known* to *God* is the *one* whose *scheme* will be *recompensed* by *God*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *scheme* is thus: the *scheme* is the *condition* of the *recompense*, and the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:47

> **So do not suppose that He would fail to keep His Promise unto His messengers. Truly God is Mighty, Possessor of Vengeance.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"So Do Not Suppose That He Would Fail to Keep His Promise unto His Messengers": The Unique Form of the Admonition**

The verse states the *admonition* of the *promise*. The clause *fa-lā taḥsabanna anna llāha mukhlifu waʿdi rusulihi* — "So do not suppose that He would fail to keep His Promise unto His messengers" — is the *word* of the *admonition*. The *commentators* note that this *verse represents the one instance in the Quran that employs an active participle* (*mukhlif*, lit. "one who breaks [a promise]") *to refer to a quality that one should never suppose of God*, thus *conveying the idea that God's Promise is always true*. The *form* of the *admonition* is the *form* of the *impossibility*: the *one* who *breaks* a *promise* is the *one* who *is not God*, and the *one* who *is not God* is the *one* who *is not the Promise*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *impossibility*: the *impossibility* of the *breaking* of the *promise* is the *condition* of the *truth* of the *promise*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *admonition* is thus: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *impossibility*, and the *impossibility* is the *condition* of the *truth* of the *promise*.

**The Sense: The Promise as Intrinsic to the Divine Nature**

The *sense* of the *admonition* is that *one should never deem God to be "a breaker of His Promise" and thus this is an emphatic statement indicating that not breaking a promise is intrinsic to the Divine Nature*. The *parallel* at 3:9 states the *same* *principle* in its *full* form:

> "Truly God will not fail the tryst." (3:9)

The *logic* of the *sense* is the *logic* of the *intrinsicness*: the *not breaking* of the *promise* is *intrinsic* to the *Divine Nature*, and the *intrinsicness* is the *condition* of the *truth* of the *promise*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *sense* is thus: the *sense* is the *condition* of the *intrinsicness*, and the *intrinsicness* is the *condition* of the *truth* of the *promise*.

**"Truly God is Mighty, Possessor of Vengeance": The Names of the Guarantee**

The *final* clause — *innā llāha ʿazīzun matīf* — "Truly God is Mighty, Possessor of Vengeance" — is the *statement* of the *guarantee*. The *word* *ʿazīzun* — "Mighty" — is the *word* of the *power*, the *word* of the *one* who *has the power* to *keep the promise*. The *word* *matīf* — "Possessor of Vengeance, the Avenger" — is the *word* of the *revenge*, the *word* of the *one* who *takes revenge* on the *one* who *breaks the covenant*. The *two* names together state the *full scope of the guarantee*: the *power* to *keep the promise* (the *Mighty*) and the *revenge* on the *one* who *breaks the covenant* (the *Avenger*). The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *guarantee*: the *guarantee* is the *condition* of the *promise*, and the *promise* is the *condition* of the *dealing*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *guarantee* is thus: the *guarantee* is the *condition* of the *promise*, and the *promise* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**The Logic: The Admonition, the Sense, and the Guarantee**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *three* *aspects* of the *promise*. The *first aspect* is the *admonition*: the *admonition* is the *condition* of the *impossibility*. The *second aspect* is the *sense*: the *sense* is the *condition* of the *intrinsicness*. The *third aspect* is the *guarantee*: the *guarantee* is the *condition* of the *promise*. The *three* aspects together state the *full scope of the promise*: the *promise* is the *admonition* (the *first*), the *sense* (the *second*), and the *guarantee* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *promise* is thus: the *promise* is the *combination* of the *three aspects*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**The Connection: The Promise of the Earlier Verses**

The *verse* *confirms* the *promise* stated in vv. 13–14: the *promise* that *God will destroy the wrongdoers* (v. 13) and *make the messengers and their followers dwell in the land after them* (v. 14). The *admonition* of v. 47 is the *confirmation* of the *promise* of vv. 13–14: the *promise* *will be kept*, and the *keeping* of the *promise* is the *condition* of the *dealing*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *confirmation* is thus: the *confirmation* is the *condition* of the *promise*, and the *promise* is the *condition* of the *dealing*.

**The Modern Application: The Reliability of the Promise**

The *verse's* teaching on the *reliability* of the *promise* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *trusts* in the *promise* of *God* is the *person* who *trusts* in the *reliability* of the *promise*. The *one* who *trusts* in the *reliability* is the *one* who *is firm* in the *faith*, and the *one* who *is firm* in the *faith* is the *one* who *is the heir* of the *land*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *trust* is thus: the *trust* is the *condition* of the *firmness*, and the *firmness* is the *condition* of the *inheritance*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:48

> **On that Day the earth shall be changed into other than the earth, and the heavens [too], and they will appear before God, the One, the Paramount.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"On That Day the Earth Shall Be Changed into Other Than the Earth": The Overturning of the Cosmic Order**

The verse states the *overturning* of the *cosmic order* on the *Day of Judgment*. The clause — "On that Day the earth shall be changed into other than the earth, and the heavens [too]" — is the *word* of the *overturning*. The *commentators* explain that the *verse refers in general terms to the overturning of the entire cosmic order on the Last Day*, and for *specific descriptions elsewhere in the Quran*, see *Sūrahs 81–83*. The *parallel* at 81:3–4 states the *overturning* in its *specific* form:

> "And when the stars are extinguished, and when the heavens are folded together." (81:2–3, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *overturning*: the *overturning* of the *earth* and the *heavens* is the *condition* of the *appearance* before *God*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *overturning* is thus: the *overturning* is the *condition* of the *appearance*, and the *appearance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Two Readings: The Change of Attributes and the Change of Substance**

The *commentators* (al-Rāzī) give *two readings* of the *change*. The *first reading*: *the change that will take place when the earth shall be changed into other than the earth, and the heavens [too] pertains only to the different attributes that comprise the earth and the heavens, but that their fundamental substance will remain unaltered*. The *second reading*: *the change in the attributes of the earth and the heavens will be so total that they will literally become something else in their substance*. The *two readings* are not in *tension*: the *change* of the *attributes* (the *first*) and the *change* of the *substance* (the *second*) are the *two* *dimensions* of the *same* *reality*: the *overturning* is *total* in its *attributes*, and the *overturning* is *total* in its *substance*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *change* is thus: the *change* is *total* in its *attributes* and *substance*, and the *totality* is the *condition* of the *overturning*.

**"They Will Appear Before God, the One, the Paramount": The Resurrection**

The *final* clause — *wa-ẓaharū li-llāhi l-wāḥidi l-qahhār* — "and they will appear before God, the One, the Paramount" — is the *statement* of the *Resurrection*. The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Jāmiʿ) explain that the *phrase refers to the Resurrection, when all people will be driven from their graves* and *brought face-to-face with God, their Lord and Master* (see also 36:51; 40:16). The *two names* — *l-wāḥid* (the *One*) and *l-qahhār* (the *Paramount*) — state the *full scope of the appearance*: the *One* is the *word* of the *unity*, and the *Paramount* is the *word* of the *subjugation*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *appearance*: the *appearance* before *God* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*, and the *reckoning* is the *condition* of the *justice*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *appearance* is thus: the *appearance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*, and the *reckoning* is the *condition* of the *justice*.

**The Logic: The Overturning and the Appearance**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *Day*. The *first aspect* is the *overturning*: the *overturning* of the *earth* and the *heavens* is the *condition* of the *appearance*. The *second aspect* is the *appearance*: the *appearance* before *God* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the Day*: the *Day* is the *overturning* (the *first*) and the *appearance* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *Day* is thus: the *Day* is the *combination* of the *overturning* and the *appearance*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Modern Application: The Transformation of the World**

The *verse's* teaching on the *transformation* of the *world* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *recognizes* the *transformation* of the *world* is the *person* who *recognizes* the *appearance* before *God*. The *one* who *recognizes* the *appearance* is the *one* who *is prepared* for the *reckoning*, and the *one* who *is prepared* for the *reckoning* is the *one* who *is just*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *transformation* is thus: the *transformation* is the *condition* of the *appearance*, and the *appearance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.

**The Frame: The Overturning and the Appearance**
