The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *overturning* and the *appearance*. The *overturning* is the *condition* of the *appearance*, and the *appearance* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the Day*: the *Day* is the *overturning* (the *first*) and the *appearance* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *Day* is thus: the *Day* is the *combination* of the *overturning* and the *appearance*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *reckoning*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:49

> **On that Day thou wilt see the guilty bound together in fetters,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"On That Day Thou Wilt See the Guilty": The Vision of the Prophet**

The verse shifts the scene from the *overturning* of the *cosmic order* (v. 48) to the *vision* of the *Prophet*. The clause *yawma tarā al-mujrimīn* — "On that Day thou wilt see the guilty" — is the *word* of the *vision*. The *address* is *direct* to the *Prophet*: *thou wilt see*. The *commentators* (al-Jāmiʿ) explain that *the guilty* is a *reference to the disbelievers*. The *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *vision*: the *vision* of the *guilty* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *vision* is thus: the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**"Bound Together in Fetters": The State of the Guilty**

The *clause* *musalsalīna fī aṣfād* — "bound together in fetters" — is the *statement* of the *state* of the *guilty*. The *word* *musalsalīna* — "bound, chained" — is the *word* of the *binding*, the *word* of the *one* who *is bound* together. The *word* *aṣfād* — "fetters, chains" — is the *word* of the *chains*, the *word* of the *one* that *binds* the *guilty* together. The *parallel* at 38:38 states the *same* *state*:

> "And the guilty bound in chains." (38:38, paraphrased)

The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *binding*: the *binding* of the *guilty* is the *condition* of the *vision*, and the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *binding* is thus: the *binding* is the *condition* of the *vision*, and the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**The Logic: The Vision and the State**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *vision*. The *first aspect* is the *vision*: the *vision* of the *guilty* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *second aspect* is the *state*: the *state* of the *guilty* is the *condition* of the *vision*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the vision*: the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning* (the *first*), and the *state* is the *condition* of the *vision* (the *second*). The *sūrah's teaching on the *nature* of the *vision* is thus: the *vision* is the *combination* of the *two aspects*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**The Modern Application: The Vision of the Guilty**

The *verse's* teaching on the *vision* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *recognizes* the *vision* of the *guilty* is the *person* who *recognizes* the *state* of the *guilty*. The *one* who *recognizes* the *state* is the *one* who *is warned*, and the *one* who *is warned* is the *one* who *responds*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *vision* is thus: the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Vision and the Warning**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *vision* and the *warning*. The *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *vision* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *vision* and the *warning*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:50

> **their garments made of pitch, and the Fire covering their faces,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Their Garments Made of Pitch": The Specificity of the Torment**

The verse continues the *description* of the *state* of the *guilty* begun in v. 49. The *clause* *ṣilāluhu min qatrān* — "their garments made of pitch" — is the *statement* of the *specificity*. The *commentators* (al-Jāmiʿ) explain that *their garments—that is, their shirts—will be made of pitch*, and the *pitch* *signifies the extreme degree to which they will be scorched in the Fire* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Jāmiʿ, al-Ṭūsī). The *word* *qatrān* — "pitch" — is the *word* of the *tar*, the *word* of the *substance* that *burns* and *scalds*. The *garments* of *pitch* are the *garments* that *burn* the *one* who *wears* them, and the *burning* of the *garments* is the *condition* of the *scorching*. The *parallel* at 76:2 states the *same* *principle*:

> "The disbelievers, whose garments are of pitch." (76:2)

The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *specificity*: the *specificity* of the *garments* is the *condition* of the *scorching*, and the *scorching* is the *condition* of the *torment*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *specificity* is thus: the *specificity* is the *condition* of the *scorching*, and the *scorching* is the *condition* of the *torment*.

**"And the Fire Covering Their Faces": The Totality of the Torment**

The *final* clause — "and the Fire covering their faces" — is the *statement* of the *totality*. The *fire* *covers* their *faces*, and the *covering* of the *faces* is the *condition* of the *totality* of the *torment*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *totality*: the *totality* of the *torment* is the *condition* of the *covering*, and the *covering* is the *condition* of the *torment*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *totality* is thus: the *totality* is the *condition* of the *covering*, and the *covering* is the *condition* of the *torment*.

**The Logic: The Specificity and the Totality**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *torment*. The *first aspect* is the *specificity*: the *specificity* of the *garments* is the *condition* of the *scorching*. The *second aspect* is the *totality*: the *totality* of the *torment* is the *condition* of the *covering*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the torment*: the *torment* is the *specificity* (the *first*) and the *totality* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *torment* is thus: the *torment* is the *combination* of the *specificity* and the *totality*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**The Modern Application: The Specificity of the Warning**

The *verse's* teaching on the *specificity* of the *warning* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *warns* with *specific* *descriptions* is the *person* whose *warning* *cannot* be *ignored*. The *one* who *warns* with *vague* *threats* is the *one* whose *warning* can be *ignored*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *warning* is thus: the *specificity* of the *description* is the *condition* of the *attention*, and the *attention* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Torment and the Warning**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *torment* and the *warning*. The *torment* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *torment* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *torment* and the *warning*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:51

> **that God may recompense every soul for that which it has earned; surely God is swift in reckoning.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"That God May Recompense Every Soul for That Which It Has Earned": The Justice of the Recompense**

The verse states the *purpose* of the *torment* described in vv. 49–50. The clause *li-yajziya llāhu kulla nafsin mā kasabat* — "that God may recompense every soul for that which it has earned" — is the *word* of the *justice*. The *commentators* explain that the *verse reiterates a Quranic theme that people will only receive what they deserve in the Hereafter, as God is in no way unjust to His servants* (see 4:40; 10:4). The *recompense* is *for* the *soul*, and the *recompense* is *for* the *earning* of the *soul*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *justice*: the *justice* of the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *earning*, and the *earning* is the *condition* of the *recompense*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *justice* is thus: the *justice* is the *condition* of the *earning*, and the *earning* is the *condition* of the *recompense*.

**"Surely God Is Swift in Reckoning": The Swiftness of the Reckoning**

The *final* clause — *innā llāha sariʿu l-ḥisāb* — "surely God is swift in reckoning" — is the *statement* of the *swiftness*. The *word* *sariʿ* — "swift" — is the *word* of the *speed*, the *word* of the *one* who *reckons* with *speed*. The *parallel* at 2:202, 3:19, 199, 5:4, 24:39, and 40:17 states the *same* *principle*: *God is swift in reckoning*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *swiftness*: the *swiftness* of the *reckoning* is the *condition* of the *justice*, and the *justice* is the *condition* of the *recompense*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *swiftness* is thus: the *swiftness* is the *condition* of the *justice*, and the *justice* is the *condition* of the *recompense*.

**The Logic: The Justice and the Swiftness**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *two* *aspects* of the *recompense*. The *first aspect* is the *justice*: the *justice* of the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *earning*. The *second aspect* is the *swiftness*: the *swiftness* of the *reckoning* is the *condition* of the *justice*. The *two* aspects together state the *full scope of the recompense*: the *recompense* is the *justice* (the *first*) and the *swiftness* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *recompense* is thus: the *recompense* is the *combination* of the *justice* and the *swiftness*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**The Modern Application: The Justice of the Account**

The *verse's* teaching on the *justice* of the *account* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *recognizes* the *justice* of the *account* is the *person* who *recognizes* the *swiftness* of the *account*. The *one* who *recognizes* the *swiftness* is the *one* who *is prepared* for the *account*, and the *one* who *is prepared* for the *account* is the *one* who *is just*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *justice* is thus: the *justice* is the *condition* of the *preparation*, and the *preparation* is the *condition* of the *justice*.

**The Frame: The Recompense and the Warning**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *recompense* and the *warning*. The *recompense* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the relationship*: the *recompense* is the *condition* of the *warning*, and the *warning* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *relationship* is thus: the *relationship* is the *combination* of the *recompense* and the *warning*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.
---

## Sūrah Ibrāhīm 14:52

> **This is a proclamation unto mankind, that they may be warned thereby, and that they may know that He is the One God, and that the possessors of intellect might be reminded.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"This Is a Proclamation unto Mankind": The Purpose of the Sūrah**

The final verse of the sūrah states the *purpose* of the *entire* sūrah. The clause *dhālika balāgh li-n-nās* — "This is a proclamation unto mankind" — is the *word* of the *purpose*. The *word* *balāgh* — "proclamation, message, reaching" — is the *word* of the *one* that *reaches* the *mankind*. The *commentators* (al-Qurṭubī) explain that *the proclamation unto mankind by which they may be warned is the Quran; it is an admonishment meant to instill fear of God's Punishment in people*. The *proclamation* is the *Qur'an*, and the *proclamation* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *purpose*: the *purpose* of the *sūrah* is the *proclamation*, and the *proclamation* is the *condition* of the *warning*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *purpose* is thus: the *purpose* is the *proclamation*, and the *proclamation* is the *condition* of the *warning*.

**"That They May Be Warned Thereby": The First Purpose — The Warning**

The *first* purpose of the *proclamation* is the *warning*: *wa-li-yunẓarū bihī* — "that they may be warned thereby." The *warning* is the *condition* of the *fear*, and the *fear* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *warning*: the *warning* is the *condition* of the *fear*, and the *fear* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *warning* is thus: the *warning* is the *condition* of the *fear*, and the *fear* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**"And That They May Know That He Is the One God": The Second Purpose — The Knowledge**

The *second* purpose of the *proclamation* is the *knowledge*: *wa-li-yaʿlamū annamā huwa ilāhun wāḥid* — "and that they may know that He is the One God." The *commentators* (al-Bayḵāwī, al-Bayḍāwī) explain that *people are meant to reflect deeply and ponder over the Quran's verses in order to understand the fact that He is the One God*. The *knowledge* is the *condition* of the *worship*, and the *worship* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *knowledge*: the *knowledge* of the *One God* is the *condition* of the *worship*, and the *worship* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *knowledge* is thus: the *knowledge* is the *condition* of the *worship*, and the *worship* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**"And That the Possessors of Intellect Might Be Reminded": The Third Purpose — The Remembrance**

The *third* purpose of the *proclamation* is the *remembrance*: *wa-li-yadhakkara ūlū l-albāb* — "and that the possessors of intellect might be reminded." The *word* *ūlū l-albāb* — "the possessors of intellect, the people of understanding" — is the *word* of the *one* who *understands*, the *one* who *reflects*, the *one* who *considers*. The *parallel* at 5:100, 2:269, 3:7, 190, 12:111, 13:19, 38:29, 43, 39:9, 18, 21, and 40:54 states the *same* *principle*: the *one* who *understands* is the *one* who *is reminded*. The *logic* of the *clause* is the *logic* of the *remembrance*: the *remembrance* is the *condition* of the *understanding*, and the *understanding* is the *condition* of the *response*. The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *remembrance* is thus: the *remembrance* is the *condition* of the *understanding*, and the *understanding* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Logic: The Three Purposes of the Proclamation**

The *logic* of the *verse*, stated in its *full* form, is a *statement* about the *three* purposes of the *proclamation*. The *first purpose* is the *warning*: the *warning* is the *condition* of the *fear*. The *second purpose* is the *knowledge*: the *knowledge* of the *One God* is the *condition* of the *worship*. The *third purpose* is the *remembrance*: the *remembrance* is the *condition* of the *understanding*. The *three* purposes together state the *full scope of the proclamation*: the *proclamation* is the *warning* (the *first*), the *knowledge* (the *second*), and the *remembrance* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *proclamation* is thus: the *proclamation* is the *combination* of the *three purposes*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Frame: The Sūrah Opens with the Book and Closes with the Purpose of the Book**

The *verse* stands in the *frame* of the *sūrah*. The *sūrah* *opens* with the *Book* that *brings* from *darkness* into *light* (v. 1), and the *sūrah* *closes* with the *purpose* of the *Book*: the *warning*, the *knowledge*, and the *remembrance*. The *two* together state the *full scope of the sūrah*: the *sūrah* is the *Book* (the *first*), and the *sūrah* is the *purpose* of the *Book* (the *second*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *nature* of the *sūrah* is thus: the *sūrah* is the *combination* of the *Book* and the *purpose* of the *Book*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *response*.

**The Modern Application: The Purpose of the Qur'an for the Modern Reader**

The *verse's* teaching on the *three purposes* of the *proclamation* is *directly* applicable to the *modern* reader. The *person* who *is warned* is the *person* who *fears*. The *person* who *knows* the *One God* is the *person* who *worships*. The *person* who *is reminded* is the *person* who *understands*. The *three* purposes together state the *full scope of the response*: the *response* is the *fear* (the *first*), the *worship* (the *second*), and the *understanding* (the *third*). The *sūrah's* teaching on the *psychology* of the *response* is thus: the *response* is the *combination* of the *three purposes*, and the *combination* is the *condition* of the *salvation*.
